# Upstream Dolphin Integration Report

_Generated 2026-10-02 for the DolphinXR fork. Audience: the DolphinXR maintainer and a later, more capable integration model that will handle the deferred commits._

## Range analyzed

- **DolphinXR starting/base revision:** `openxr-work` @ `5e9a304a65` — merge-base with upstream is `b55aaa8ca7` (2026-02-21, "Merge pull request #14376 from Windsurf7/lozss-gameini")
- **Upstream target revision:** `771fb15405` = `upstream/master` on 2026-09-30 ("Merge pull request #14870 from LillyJadeKatrin/retroachievements-allowlist-update")
- **Number of upstream commits analyzed:** 556 non-merge commits, in 354 landing groups (344 PR merges, 7 direct commits, 3 release-branch merges)
- **Number integrated:** 272 in stage 1, **453 after stage 2** (see "Stage 2" below)
- **Number skipped:** 284

| Outcome | Commits |
|---|---:|
| Integrated | 272 |
| Medium risk | 125 |
| Blocked by dependency | 76 |
| High risk | 62 |
| Potentially obsolete | 21 |

## Where the integrated work is

- **Branch:** `upstream-integration` = `openxr-work` @ `5e9a304a65` + 272 cherry-picked upstream commits + this report. It is **not pushed**, and `openxr-work` itself was **not modified**.
- **Worktree:** `C:\Users\Christophe\Documents\DXR-upstream` (a separate `git worktree` of the main repo). It has its own `Build-vs2022/` (same CMake options as the main `Build-vs2022`: VS2022 x64, `ENABLE_VR=ON`) and its own `Binary/`, so the VR binary in the main checkout was never overwritten.
- **Provenance:** every integrated commit is a `git cherry-pick -x` of the upstream commit. The upstream author and author date are preserved, and the message ends with `(cherry picked from commit <upstream hash>)`. The 9 commits that needed the documented `STATE_VERSION` renumbering also carry a "DolphinXR integration:" note and a `Co-Authored-By` trailer.
- **Adopting it:** if `openxr-work` has not moved since `5e9a304a65`, run `git merge --ff-only upstream-integration` from the main checkout. Otherwise run `git merge upstream-integration`; conflicts are only possible in files you have changed since.
- **Cleanup when done:** `git worktree remove C:\Users\Christophe\Documents\DXR-upstream` (it holds ~10 GB of build output). Its submodules are local `--shared` clones of the main repo's module stores, so nothing was downloaded. `Externals/OpenXR` in the worktree is checked out at the same nested commit as the main checkout (`d6fe963`). The superproject gitlink for it (`a170408`) is unchanged; that commit does not exist locally.
- **Machine-readable data** (per-commit decisions, classifier and skeptic reasoning, simulations, logs): `C:\Users\Christophe\Documents\DXR-upstream-data\` (outside both working trees).

## Stage 2 (2026-10-13): medium-risk pass

At the maintainer's request the remaining commits were then integrated in upstream landing order on the same branch, taking every MEDIUM commit and every commit blocked only by MEDIUM work. The remaining HIGH groups and their dependents are deferred to a later pass. Commits were applied with the same `cherry-pick -x` driver, and every conflict was resolved by hand, keeping XR code and applying upstream's intent.

| Stage-2 outcome | Commits |
|---|---:|
| Integrated, clean cherry-pick | 159 |
| Integrated, manual conflict resolution | 16 |
| Integrated, STATE_VERSION renumbering only | 6 |
| Skipped as obsolete (release-branch duplicates, commit+revert pair, superseded R8 pin, XR-equivalent abiFilters) | 19 |
| Deferred to the HIGH pass | 84 |

**Totals after stage 2:** 453 of 556 upstream commits integrated, 19 obsolete, 84 deferred. `STATE_VERSION` is now **196**. The version string now follows upstream (**2609.0**), because all four ScmRevGen bumps were taken in order.

### Notes and corrections

- **Correction to key finding 4 (G152, Metroid Prime 2):** the commit only cleans up comments and boilerplate in `G2M.ini`, `G2ME01.ini` and `G2MP01.ini`. `EFBToTextureEnable = False` was **already** present in `G2M.ini` at XR's baseline, so it changes nothing for VR. It is integrated.
- **Custom crop (G176/G178)** applies to the desktop present paths only: `RenderXFBToScreen`, `ShowImage` and window sizing. XR's `#ifdef ENABLE_VR` presentation blocks keep their own frame-region and cinematic-bar logic, so the headset view is unaffected. The option is off by default.
- **G079 (zero-stride EFB/XFB copies, HIGH)** and **G063 (Android version catalog + AGP 9, HIGH)** were integrated in this pass, because they came early in landing order. For G079, the diff of the resolved `BPStructs.cpp` against upstream's post-commit file consists only of XR's additions. G063 builds for Quest.
- **XR preservation audit (whitespace-normalised)** over all 377 XR-touched files: every XR-added line is still present except intended rewrites. Those are the `STATE_VERSION` line; `State::Save(..., int slot, SaveOptions)`, now `u32 slot` with `SaveOptions` kept; the `EGLHelper` method renames inside XR's `IS_QUEST` condition; the Android 5.0→7.0 Readme line; `StartupHandler.java`, which became `StartupHandler.kt` with XR's `FirstLaunchDialogs.checkInit` hook; and the `UpdateStereoscopyAvailability()` call inside the removed `EnhancementsWidget::OnConfigChanged()`, which is still covered by XR's `Settings::ConfigChanged` connection and the `OnBackendChanged()` call.

## Key findings for the next integrator

1. **Toolchain gates block whole areas; decide these first.**
   - `G314` *Require VS2026* (HIGH): `Source/PCH/pch.h` gains a hard `_MSC_FULL_VER` floor of `195136252` (VS2026). This machine builds with VS2022 (MSVC `19.44.35215`), so cherry-picking it breaks the Windows build until VS2026 is installed.
   - `G207` *bundled Windows Qt 6.8.3* (HIGH). XR builds against `Externals/Qt/Qt6.5.1`. `G340` (QtPaths) and `G293` (windeployqt) wait on it.
   - `G273` *CMake: Replace the Visual Studio project* (HIGH, 11 commits). XR does not use the `.sln`/`.vcxproj` files, but the PR rewrites root CMake/presets and the Android Gradle CMake invocation. `G276` (CMake version gates) builds on it.
   - Android toolchain chain: `G041` minSdk 24 → `G063` AGP 9 + version catalog (HIGH) → `G246` dependency/AGP update (HIGH). `G067` (R8 pin) is obsolete because `G246` deletes it again. `G223` *Android netplay* (46 commits, HIGH, Jetpack Compose) sits on top of `G063`/`G041`/`G120`, and 11 more Android commits wait on it.
2. **Every VR-critical renderer change was deferred.** These are `G079` (zero-stride EFB/XFB copies in `BPStructs`), `G327` (palette/reinterpret texture split, which overlaps the Metroid visor palette fixes in `TextureCacheBase`), `G335` (vertex-shader UID shrink to 28 bytes, which collides with XR's VR UID bits in `VertexShaderGen`), `G144` (remove GLX, which XR's OpenGL OpenXR backend still uses on Linux), `G097` (graphics-mod EFB materials inside `CopyRenderTargetToTexture`), `G176`/`G178`/`G179`/`G235` (screen crop, aspect crop and internal-resolution stats in `Present.cpp` and XR's OSD pane), `G122` (+`G124`, `G146` *StereoMode rename*, `G219`), `G095`, `G013` and `G032`/`G038`. Each section below names the exact XR functions involved and a porting plan. Each one also needs a headset test, not just a build.
3. **Savestates: STATE_VERSION renumbering policy.** Upstream bumped `STATE_VERSION` 15 times (177→192), while XR had moved to 181 for its own VR state. Each integrated upstream bump was resolved as *XR current + 1*, and the comment records the upstream number. XR is now at **190** after 9 integrated bumps. Every deferred savestate change still needs the same +1 renumbering when integrated: `G193` (NAND savestating rewrite, which `G303` and `G344` fix up later), `G213`, `G172`, `G086` (+`G105`, `G123`), and others.
4. **INI changes for VR-profiled or Metroid titles were held for device testing.** `G152` turns off EFB-to-texture for Metroid Prime 2 (Wii). That forces EFB copies to RAM, which are mono in XR, so the stereo visor effects would break. Also held: `G000` (Rogue Squadron, GSWE64 VR profile: EFB-to-RAM removal plus `CPUCull`), `G153` (Sonic and the Secret Rings), `G154` (Simpsons Hit & Run, ImmediateXFB), `G220` (`FastDepthCalc=False`, a VR depth regression) and `G119` (blocked by `G079`). INI changes for non-VR titles were integrated. The ones that touch EFB/XFB keys are listed after the integrated table.
5. **Adversarial review caught real problems that compile and pass tests.** `G120` (`std::vector&`→`std::span`) has an upstream span-lifetime bug: queued Qt lambdas capture spans whose vectors are gone when the lambda runs. `G285` (ELF load address) also changes the IOS boot loader path, not only homebrew ELFs. `G150`, `G303` and `G338` have hidden prerequisites on deferred commits. Details are in each section under "Adversarial-review downgrades".
6. **Security hardening that is waiting on a refactor.** The DolReader fixes `G259` → `G281` → `G311` → `G319` are gated on `G120`, because `G311` needs the `DolReader::Initialize(std::span<const u8>)` signature. Applying **only G120's two DolReader hunks** (files XR never touched) unblocks the chain without taking G120's Qt lifetime bug. The GCZ hardening in `G296` needs `G139`'s context.
7. **Version numbers are a fork-policy decision.** XR still reports **2512.0**. Upstream bumped 2512→2603 (`G020`), →2606 (`G202`), →2606a→2609 (inside `G349`). These one-line `ScmRevGen.cmake` commits were deliberately not applied, because claiming "2609" with only part of 2609's changes would mislead users, NetPlay version checks and the updater. Decide on a DolphinXR versioning scheme first.

## Method

1. **Range.** `git merge-base openxr-work upstream/master` = `b55aaa8ca7`. The 556 non-merge upstream commits were grouped by the first-parent merge that landed them (354 groups) and ordered by landing order, which is the order upstream applied them.
2. **Mechanical evidence per commit** (computed with `git merge-tree` only, so no working tree or ref was touched): overlap with the 389 files XR modified outside `docs/`, a cherry-pick of the commit alone onto XR HEAD, a greedy sequential replay of all of upstream onto XR HEAD and onto the pure merge-base, and earlier upstream commits touching the same files.
3. **Classification.** 54 subsystem batches each went to a classifier agent that read the real diffs and XR's own edits in every overlapping file, using a written rubric (LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/BLOCKED, with Android, INI, toolchain and version-bump rules).
4. **Adversarial verification.** Every LOW verdict was attacked by skeptic agents. Sensitive batches (video, Android, input, build, cross-cutting) got two independent lenses: XR-interaction, and dependencies/build. A LOW survived only if every skeptic upheld it with concrete checks such as blob identity, JNI descriptors, compiled interop or scratch applies. 5 LOWs were refuted.
5. **Integrability rules.** PRs were kept atomic, except release-branch bundles. Dependencies named by a classifier or skeptic were closed transitively, so a commit that needs a deferred commit is BLOCKED. Obsolete and duplicate commits are never applied.
6. **Exact pre-check.** The final 272-commit plan was replayed with `git merge-tree` plus the scripted `STATE_VERSION` resolution. The real cherry-pick run produced a tree that is **byte-identical** to the simulated one (`460cc688`).
7. **Preservation audit.** All 52,205 non-trivial lines that DolphinXR added in 378 files are still present after integration. The only exception is the renumbered `STATE_VERSION` line. XR's `fmt` and `OpenXR` gitlinks are unchanged.
8. **Builds and tests**, before and after integration: see "Build/test results".

## Successfully integrated commits

These 272 commits are in branch `upstream-integration`, in upstream landing order. They cover game INIs for titles without VR profiles, all 7 Transifex syncs, Triforce/AM-baseboard emulation, GBA-core fixes, DiscIO/IOS/NetPlay/DSP/EXI security hardening, Jit64/JitArm64 fixes, Qt debugger and UI widgets XR does not modify, InputCommon backend fixes (DSU, GC adapter, SDL gamepad), SDL 3.4.0→3.4.12 and cpp-ipc submodule updates, a set of Android Java→Kotlin conversions whose JNI and caller compatibility was verified, and small build fixes.

"Applied" is either `clean` (unmodified cherry-pick) or the single documented *trivial resolution*: the `STATE_VERSION` line only, set to XR's current value + 1.

**Build/test status for every integrated commit:** built and tested **once, with all 272 commits applied** (not per commit). Windows VS2022 Release with `ENABLE_VR=ON` built with 0 errors and no new compiler warnings. All 1376 unit tests pass. The Quest `assembleQuestDebug` APK builds. **Not runtime-tested**: no game was booted and no headset test was run.

### Integrated INI changes that touch EFB/XFB/video settings (non-VR titles; worth a glance)

- G015 GameSettings: Add patches to limit the internal frame rate in Rabbids Go Home and Rabbids Lab.: WR2.ini drops ImmediateXFBEnable=False; it's not a VR title. In XR the Immediate XFB state decides VR pose locking (VRLockHeadPosePerFrame = !bImmediateXFB), so Rabbids Lab in VR now follows the user's global Immediate XFB setting. Later G143 (4b042bd3d0) adds RGWP41.ini and G239 (a5f6ba81fa) edits the files created here.
- G017 GameSettings: Add patches for Need for Speed: Hot Pursuit 2 to limit the internal frame rate.: Deleting GH2.ini means ImmediateXFB now follows the global setting for NFS HP2 (not a VR title). In XR that decides VR pose locking (VRLockHeadPosePerFrame = !bImmediateXFB) when the game is played in VR. Later G239 (a5f6ba81fa) edits GH2E69/GH2P69.
- G126 GameSettings: EFBAccessEnable for "NFL Street 2" to fix broken custom character portraits.: EFB setting changed for a non-VR title (GN7 EFBAccessEnable=True). EFB access means synchronous GPU readbacks. On XR's D3D12+PSVR2 path each readback fence takes about 6.9 ms (a known XR issue), so NFL Street 2 in VR on that combination may lose frames; Vulkan is fine.
- G127 GameSettings: Disable DeferEFBCopies to fix shadows in "Disney's Hide & Sneak".: EFB copy setting changed for non-VR titles GAA and GHV. With DeferEFBCopies off, every EFB-to-RAM copy flushes and syncs the GPU, so these games may run slower in VR. The games already use EFB-to-RAM copies (EFBToTextureEnable=False), and those copies are mono in XR.
- G128 GameSettings: Disable ImmediateXFB for "Who Wants To Be A Millionaire".: XFB setting changed for non-VR title SMI (ImmediateXFBEnable=False). On Quest this changes behaviour relative to XR's Immediate-XFB-on default, which is the intended effect. On PC, where the default is already off, it only affects users who enabled Immediate XFB.
- G129 GameSettings: Enable VertexRounding in "Shrek Smash n' Crash Racing".: Vertex-rounding setting changed for non-VR title G4I. The vertical-line artefact may still appear in VR, because XR suppresses vertex rounding in OpenXR stereo mode. UseVertexRounding() also needs an internal resolution other than 1x.
- G135 GameSettings: Apply settings for alternate regions of "MySims Agents".: EFB copy setting changed for non-VR title R5X (EFBToTextureEnable=False). In XR, EFB copies that go through RAM are mono; only EFB-to-texture copies are stereo 2-layer. The title already behaves this way under its US ID (R6Q).
- G136 GameSettings: Various fitness game fixes.: EFB setting changed for non-VR titles REU and SM6 (EFBAccessEnable=True). The same D3D12+PSVR2 EFB readback cost caveat applies as for G126. RU4 and SLT change only texture-cache accuracy.
- G138 GameSettings: Add comments with the reason for disabling ImmediateXFB for many games.: XFB settings changed for non-VR titles: ImmediateXFBEnable=False added for RUA, S5M and SU5. EFB setting changed for RSO (EFBAccessEnable=True). R8L loses SuggestedAspectRatio=2. This belongs to the same upstream ImmediateXFB INI campaign as G119, G128 and G154, but it has no code dependency.
- G183 GameSettings: Add Photo Channel: Forces EFB copies to RAM and full texture hashing for HAAA01, HAYA01 and HAYK01. That costs some performance and has no VR-profile impact.
- G190 GameSettings: Apply settings required for Solitaire and Mahjong WiiWare.: EFBAccessEnable=True enables CPU EFB peeks. XR's D3D12+PSVR2 EFB-readback latency caveat applies (performance only).
- G203 GameSettings: fix pony not showing up in Pony Friends 2: EFB setting change on a game without a VR profile (R2R*): EFBToTextureEnable=False means copies go to RAM, which are mono in XR's stereo pipeline. Pure upstream accuracy fix with no effect on profiled games.
- G217 GameSettings: Add Wind Waker pre-release demo: EFB settings on a game without a VR profile (D44J01): EFBAccessEnable=True means EFB peeks, and XR's measured PSVR2+D3D12 EFB-access stall applies just as it does for GZL. EFBToTextureEnable=False means mono EFB-to-RAM copies in VR. The soft dependency on G122 is cosmetic and only matters when GPU Texture Decoding is enabled (not the default).
- G221 GameSettings: Don't force immediate XFB off in Ultimate Spider-Man.: XFB INI change on a game without a VR profile (GUTD52/E52/F52/I52/JC0/P52/S52). It belongs to the same independent ImmediateXFB sweep as G119/G128/G138/G154.

## Medium-risk commits requiring review

Not integrated automatically. Upstream's intent is clear and usually the conflict surface is small, but one of these applies: the commit touches XR-modified code or behavior (rendering, config layers, input plumbing, Quest/Android), it changes a toolchain/dependency, it needs a VR device test, or it is a policy decision (version bumps, contributor policy). Many apply cleanly; the "suggested approach" says so where it does.

64 groups, 119 commits.

### G000 — PR #14331 — GameSettings: Update Rogue Squadron II and III

- **Subsystem:** GameSettings (Data/Sys/GameSettings INI)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-02-23 (merge `ffa03fec78`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Data/Sys/GameSettings/DLS.ini`, `Data/Sys/GameSettings/GLR.ini`, `Data/Sys/GameSettings/GSW.ini`
- **XR-modified files touched:** none
- **Upstream intent:** Overhaul of the INIs for RS2 (GSW.ini, Rogue Leader), RS3 (GLR.ini) and the RS3 demo (DLS.ini), with a comment on every setting. Keeps MMU. RS3 gets CPUThread=False (dual core freezes) and SuggestedAspectRatio=2. RS2 gets JITFollowBranch=False. All three get CPUCull=True for performance. It drops EFBToTextureEnable=False (EFB-to-RAM) from all three, EFBEmulateFormatChanges=True from GSW and DeferEFBCopies=False from GLR, keeps ImmediateXFBEnable=False, and removes the empty boilerplate sections.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** Rule 7: GSW.ini covers GSWE64, which has an XR VR profile (Data/Sys/GameSettingsVR/GSWE64.ini), and the commit changes its EFB copy settings. (1) Dropping EFBToTextureEnable=False sets bSkipEFBCopyToRam=true. TextureCacheBase.cpp ~L2470 (copy_to_ram = !bSkipEFBCopyToRam \|\| !copy_to_vram) then keeps copies in VRAM only, where XR renders them as 2-layer stereo. Before, they could be re-decoded from RAM as mono. (2) EFBEmulateFormatChanges goes back to its default (off). (3) CPUCull=True is added. CPUCullImpl.h CullTriangle culls against the game's own projection (vsmanager.constants.projection, +-w clip test), so with head tracking, geometry just outside the game's frustum can disappear at the headset's field-of-view edges. XR only bypasses CPU culling when the VR profile sets DisableCPUCull (VertexLoaderManager.cpp cpu_cull_allowed_in_vr / GFX_VR_DISABLE_CPU_CULL), and GSWE64.ini does not set it. Precedent: upstream CPUCull=True in GM8.ini and RM3.ini made XR add DisableCPUCull=True to its GM8E01 and RM3E01 profiles. Textually trivial: merge-tree onto 5e9a304a65 is clean, XR never modified Data/Sys/GameSettings, and GSWE64.ini upstream holds only Gecko codes, so the GSW.ini values take effect.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** No file overlap. Behavior overlap: Data/Sys/GameSettingsVR/GSWE64.ini (VirtualScreen=False; ElementsGroupOverride $Minimap/$Ship wireframe/$Visor/$HUD, matched by viewport/scissor/render-state signatures, all handling=screen). VertexLoaderManager.cpp cpu_cull_allowed_in_vr gate plus ConfigManager.cpp ApplyGameVRConfigOverrides 'DisableCPUCull'. TextureCacheBase.cpp stereo EFB-copy path (copy_to_ram/copy_to_vram) and the AutoNativeEfbEffects routing of ortho draws that sample EFB copies. GLR/DLS (RS3) have no VR profile.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick 2891bc9495 as-is; it applies cleanly. Then test Rogue Leader (GSWE64) in a headset on PC (D3D11/Vulkan) and Quest. (a) Turn your head in flight and over terrain and watch for triangles popping at the edges of the view (CPUCull). If they appear, set the existing per-game knob DisableCPUCull=True under [Graphics.VR] in GameSettingsVR/GSWE64.ini, as GM8E01/RM3E01 already do; no code change. (b) Check that the four element overrides still match and route correctly now that EFB copies stay in VRAM. The Visor/HUD ortho draws may now be caught by AutoNativeEfbEffects. (c) Check menus, fades and the targeting computer in stereo. If a VR regression can't be fixed through the profile, split the commit: take the GLR.ini/DLS.ini hunks and hold the GSW.ini hunk.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **INI video-setting change:** yes (EFB/XFB/copy/vertex-rounding settings)
- **Notes:** The RS3 files (GLR/DLS, no VR profile) also drop EFB-to-RAM and DeferEFBCopies=False and force single core (CPUThread=False); these are performance changes only. Upstream says dropping EFB-to-RAM turns some RS2 fadeouts pure black, and that applies in VR too. No later in-range upstream commit touches GSW/GLR/DLS.
- **Commits:**
  - `2891bc9495` GameSettings: Update Rogue Squadron II and III — **MEDIUM**: Changes EFBToTextureEnable, EFBEmulateFormatChanges and adds CPUCull=True for VR-profiled GSWE64; clean apply; needs a VR device test.

### G006 — PR #14415 — Android: Fix defaults for RetroAchievements settings

- **Subsystem:** Android app / settings model (RetroAchievements)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-02-24 (merge `2a3078b833`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Android/app/src/main/java/org/dolphinemu/dolphinemu/features/settings/model/BooleanSetting.kt`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Source/Android/app/src/main/java/org/dolphinemu/dolphinemu/features/settings/model/BooleanSetting.kt`
- **Upstream intent:** Make the Kotlin BooleanSetting defaults match the C++ Config defaults. ACHIEVEMENTS_HARDCORE_ENABLED changes false->true and ACHIEVEMENTS_PROGRESS_ENABLED changes true->false; C++ has RA_HARDCORE_ENABLED=true and RA_PROGRESS_ENABLED=false in AchievementSettings.cpp. After this, a setting the user never changed shows the value the core actually uses.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** It applies cleanly both alone and in the greedy run. Its two hunks (~L895 and ~L931) don't overlap XR's only edits in this file. XR never touched AchievementSettings.cpp, so the fix is also correct for XR. But BooleanSetting.kt is an XR-modified Android file (rule 6), and changing a default is not behavior-neutral (rule 2), so it cannot be LOW. It is a trivial MEDIUM.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** In BooleanSetting.kt, XR adds `import org.dolphinemu.dolphinemu.BuildConfig` (L5) and changes the GFX_HACK_IMMEDIATE_XFB ("ImmediateXFBEnable") default to `BuildConfig.IS_QUEST` (~L852). Upstream only edits the ACHIEVEMENTS_HARDCORE_ENABLED and ACHIEVEMENTS_PROGRESS_ENABLED defaults, so the two sets of edits don't interact. XR's SettingsFragmentPresenter.kt uses these two settings only in upstream's untouched achievements section (L1075/L1123).
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick 99d82829c8 as-is; it applies cleanly. In the result, confirm XR's BuildConfig import and the `BuildConfig.IS_QUEST` ImmediateXFBEnable default are still there. Build questDebug and standardDebug. Optionally, on a fresh profile, check that Settings > Achievements shows Hardcore ON and Progress OFF.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Notes:** It is MEDIUM only because of rule 6 (XR-modified Android file). There is no JNI or native change and no VR interaction.
- **Commits:**
  - `99d82829c8` Android: Fix defaults for RetroAchievements settings — **MEDIUM**: A 2-line default change in XR-modified BooleanSetting.kt that doesn't overlap XR's edits. It applies cleanly and aligns Kotlin with the unchanged C++ defaults.

### G013 — PR #14432 — VertexLoaderManager: Fix crash caused by invalid array base

- **Subsystem:** VideoCommon/VertexLoaderManager (CP vertex array base caching)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-02 (merge `5c62c90d35`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Core/VideoCommon/VertexLoaderManager.cpp`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Source/Core/VideoCommon/VertexLoaderManager.cpp`
- **Upstream intent:** Change how UpdateVertexArrayPointers() resolves the Position/Normal/Color/TexCoord array bases: use memory.GetPointerForRange(base, sizeof(u32)) instead of GetSpanForAddress(base).data(). When a game sets an invalid base, keep the previously cached base instead of storing a null pointer that crashes the vertex loaders.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** Applies cleanly, alone and in greedy order. The only hunk is in UpdateVertexArrayPointers(), which is separate from XR's edits in the same file. It is not LOW only because of rubric rule 2: this is a behavioral bug fix, not a cosmetic cleanup, inside an XR-modified VideoCommon file on the FIFO/vertex-loading path. Practical risk is very low. Valid addresses give exactly the same pointer as before. Only invalid bases behave differently: they are kept instead of nulled. GetPointerForRange can also raise an 'Oversized range' PanicAlert when a base sits in the last <4 bytes of a memory region.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** Source/Core/VideoCommon/VertexLoaderManager.cpp. XR adds #include "VideoCommon/HideObjectEngine.h". In RunVertices<> it adds a HideObjectEngine::ShouldHide(src) early return and a CaptureVertexPrefix() call, plus the OpenXR CPU-cull opt-out (g_ActiveConfig.vr_disable_cpu_cull gating can_cpu_cull). Upstream only edits UpdateVertexArrayPointers(). HideObjectEngine matches raw FIFO vertex bytes (the indices), not the array data, so there is no interaction. cached_arraybases is also consumed by VertexLoaderARM64 (Quest) through MOVP2R of the array address, which is unchanged.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick edb576f4c0 as-is; no conflict is expected and no XR change is needed. GetPointerForRange already exists at XR HEAD (Memmap.cpp:618). Build Build-vs2022 (ENABLE_VR=ON) and the Quest flavor. Smoke-test one VR-profiled game with Hide Objects codes active and with CPU cull on and off.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Notes:** This would be LOW if the file were not XR-modified. XR has not fixed this already: HEAD still uses GetSpanForAddress(...).data() with the old TODO comment.
- **Commits:**
  - `edb576f4c0` VertexLoaderManager: Fix crash caused by invalid array base — **MEDIUM**: Same as the group: a defensive crash fix in XR-modified VertexLoaderManager.cpp, with its hunk separate from XR's RunVertices edits.

### G018 — PR #14438 — DolphinQt: Save when closing settings window

- **Subsystem:** DolphinQt settings persistence (SettingsWindow / GeneralPane / WiiPane)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-04 (merge `49d5299f1e`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Config/SettingsWindow.cpp`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Config/SettingsWindow.h`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Settings/GeneralPane.cpp`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Settings/WiiPane.cpp`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Config/SettingsWindow.cpp`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Config/SettingsWindow.h`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Settings/GeneralPane.cpp`
- **Upstream intent:** Save the config when the main Settings dialog closes. This adds SettingsWindow::closeEvent -> Config::Save(). It also drops the ad-hoc immediate saves: Config::Save() in the speed-limit combo lambda, SConfig::SaveSettings() in GeneralPane::OnSaveConfig (plus the now-unused Core/ConfigManager.h include) and Config::Save() after a WiiPane USB whitelist add. Settings then survive crashes and shutdown hangs.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** Applies cleanly both alone and in the greedy run (I re-checked with merge-tree). The XR-modified files are only touched in hunks disjoint from XR's edits. In SettingsWindow.cpp/.h upstream appends closeEvent at the end of the file and class. In GeneralPane.cpp it edits ConnectLayout and OnSaveConfig, while XR's edits sit in CreateBasic and AddDescriptions. It is still a behavior change (when config is saved) inside XR-modified files, so rubric rule 2 rules out LOW. I found no VR interaction. VRPane and VRConfigWidget never call Config::Save, and nothing in XR connects to SettingsWindow's finished/rejected signals. The override does not call QDialog::closeEvent, so closing with the title-bar X no longer emits rejected(); XR does not depend on that. Config::Save() writes the same base-layer values the SConfig destructor already writes at exit, just earlier. That includes XR's runtime SetBase writes of GFX_VR_FORCED_VBI_FREQUENCY and GFX_VR_AUTO_VBI_FROM_HMD, and SetBaseOrCurrent(GFX_VR_PASSTHROUGH,false). It also fires OnConfigChanged once, the same as any setting change. XR's GeneralPane has no other SConfig use, so removing the ConfigManager.h include is safe.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** SettingsWindow.cpp: XR adds #include VRPane.h and AddWrappedPane(new VRPane, tr("VR")) in the SettingsWindow constructor. SettingsWindow.h: XR adds SettingsWindowPaneIndex::VR. GeneralPane.cpp: XR adds the 'Emulate Disc Speed' ConfigBool (MAIN_FAST_DISC_SPEED) in CreateBasic and AddDescriptions. All disjoint from upstream's hunks. Side effect: VR pane settings are now also persisted when the Settings window closes (good).
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick d25ef67d6f as-is; it is clean. Build Build-vs2022 (ENABLE_VR=ON). Smoke test: change a General setting and a VR pane setting, close Settings with the title-bar X, kill Dolphin, and confirm Dolphin.ini/GFX.ini kept the values. Do not add fork-specific save logic.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Notes:** MEDIUM only because of rubric rule 2 (non-neutral change in XR-modified files); in practice it is safe. Possible caveat, not verified: on Qt>=6.3, QDialog::done() (Close button / Esc -> reject) may suppress the QCloseEvent, which would mean only a window-manager close triggers the save. XR's Qt 6.5.1 and upstream's 6.8.3 would behave the same either way, so it is not a fork issue. G091 (864ba85a50) touches the same GeneralPane.cpp but applies independently.
- **Commits:**
  - `d25ef67d6f` DolphinQt: Save when closing settings window — **MEDIUM**: Clean, disjoint hunks in XR-modified SettingsWindow.cpp/.h and GeneralPane.cpp, but it changes when config is saved (rule 2). No VR interaction found.

### G020 — release-branch merge — Merge branch 'release-prep-2603'

- **Subsystem:** Build / versioning (CMake/ScmRevGen.cmake)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-12 (merge `899124466c`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `CMake/ScmRevGen.cmake`
- **XR-modified files touched:** none
- **Upstream intent:** Release-prep merge for upstream release 2603: changes set(DOLPHIN_VERSION_MAJOR "2512") to "2603" in CMake/ScmRevGen.cmake. Upstream tag `2603` points at this exact commit. Merge 899124466c brings only this commit (^p1..p2 = d8558142ea), its net diff against the first parent is this one line, and its combined diff is empty, so it is not an evil merge.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** Rubric rule 8: DOLPHIN_VERSION bumps are a fork-policy decision, so MEDIUM. The patch itself is trivial and clean. XR never modified ScmRevGen.cmake (the blob at XR HEAD is 73ebb4043b, the same as MB and as this commit's parent), so it applies cleanly alone and in greedy order. It has almost no effect on XR's git-built binaries. SCM_DESC_STR (title bar, About, NetPlay version via GetNetplayDolphinVer, user agent, Android GetScmRevStr JNI at MainAndroid.cpp:464) comes from `git describe`, which currently gives 2512-590, and not from this variable. With a git checkout, scmrev.h.in has no input from this variable, and copy_if_different leaves scmrev.h unchanged, so nothing recompiles. The variable is read only by four things: the no-git fallback describe string, CPack version (CMakeLists.txt:908-910), the macOS VersionInfo.plist, and the Flatpak fallback. OpenXR app info is hardcoded (OpenXRManager.cpp:540/542, applicationVersion/engineVersion = 1). The updater uses the git hash (AutoUpdate.cpp:197-198). Android versionName/versionCode come from git describe and rev-list in build.gradle.kts.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** none. XR never touched CMake/ScmRevGen.cmake. The only nearby XR edit is CMakeLists.txt, where XR added set(CPACK_PACKAGE_CONTACT "dolphinxr@example.com") right above the CPACK_PACKAGE_VERSION_* lines that read DOLPHIN_VERSION_MAJOR. That is fork packaging identity, which is why the version number is a policy choice.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Make one fork-versioning decision covering the whole upstream bump chain: d8558142ea (G020, 2603) -> 6094cfcf7b (G202, 2606) -> 596d71fbba (G349, 2606a, MINOR 0->1) -> f84df02055 (G349, 2609.0, which is the upstream target value). (a) To track upstream numbering: cherry-pick all four in landing order (each applies cleanly on top of the previous: blob 73ebb4043b -> 93956b2eed -> baa2d0ae41 -> ...), or set MAJOR "2609" / MINOR "0" once at the end. Cherry-picks do not make upstream tags 2603/2606/2609 reachable, so `git describe` (shown version, Android versionName) stays at 2512-N and will not match the CPack/fallback version unless XR adds its own tag or integrates through a real merge. (b) To keep XR's own scheme: skip all four. Nothing else depends on them. Either way, only a CMake reconfigure of Build-vs2022 (ENABLE_VR=ON) and the Quest build is needed to confirm it.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Handle together with:** G202 (Merge branch 'release-prep-2606'), G349 (Merge branch 'release-prep-2609')
- **Notes:** The link to G349 covers only its two ScmRevGen commits (596d71fbba, f84df02055). G349's other commits (DSPHLE, GCZ, DolReader, ELF, NetPlay LZO, IOS/FS) are separate decisions. There is no Android, INI, STATE_VERSION or toolchain change, and no effect on VS2022/Qt6.5.1/NDK. A `2603a` tag exists upstream, but no 2603a bump commit is in the MB..target range.
- **Commits:**
  - `d8558142ea` ScmRevGen: Bump version to 2603 — **MEDIUM**: Single-line DOLPHIN_VERSION_MAJOR 2512->2603 change. It applies cleanly (XR never touched the file) and does not change any code behavior, but rubric rule 8 makes version bumps a fork-policy decision.

### G027 — PR #14426 — Android: Convert AfterDirectoryInitializationRunner to Kotlin

- **Subsystem:** Android app (startup / directory-initialization gating)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-12 (merge `ff84a4b07a`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Android/app/src/main/java/org/dolphinemu/dolphinemu/utils/AfterDirectoryInitializationRunner.java`, `Source/Android/app/src/main/java/org/dolphinemu/dolphinemu/utils/AfterDirectoryInitializationRunner.kt`
- **XR-modified files touched:** none
- **Upstream intent:** Mechanically convert AfterDirectoryInitializationRunner from Java to Kotlin. The public API is identical: runWithLifecycle(LifecycleOwner, Runnable), runWithoutLifecycle(Runnable) and cancel(). The observer is now nullable, and cancel() skips removeObserver when no observer was registered (the Java version effectively did nothing too).
- **Why not integrated automatically:** The file itself is not XR-modified and applies cleanly. But XR-modified and XR-added Android files call this class (rule 6). The XR-added FirstLaunchDialogs.kt L13 uses it for the Quest controller-setup first-launch dialog. XR-modified MainPresenter.kt uses it in XR's new `R.id.menu_quest_controller_presets` handler. Upstream call sites also sit inside XR-modified files: EmulationActivity.kt L1149, SettingsActivityPresenter.kt L76, EmulationFragment.kt L105, MainActivity.kt and StartupHandler.java L125. I checked every argument: all are non-null (FragmentActivity, AppCompatActivity lateinit, `this`). Kotlin SAM conversion for java.lang.Runnable keeps the trailing-lambda calls compiling. So it should build unchanged, but it is MEDIUM because it touches the class XR's Quest first-launch flow depends on.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** No file overlap. These XR call sites depend on the runner: FirstLaunchDialogs.checkInit() (XR-added, which gates QuestControllerSetupDialog vs Analytics) and MainPresenter's menu_quest_controller_presets -> showQuestControllerSetupDialog (XR-added). Upstream call sites also sit inside XR-modified EmulationActivity.performLaunchChecks, SettingsActivityPresenter.prepareDolphinDirectoriesIfNeeded, EmulationFragment.onResume, MainActivity and StartupHandler.HandleInit (which XR changed to call FirstLaunchDialogs.checkInit).
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick 79f0c814e4 as-is; it applies cleanly and no call-site edits are needed. Build questDebug and standardDebug. On the Quest, smoke-test a fresh install: the controller-setup dialog should appear after directory init, then analytics. Also check the Quest controller presets menu item and that launching a game still works through performLaunchChecks.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Notes:** Later, G180 (7ef2906351, DirectoryInitialization.java -> Kotlin, conflicts with XR's DirectoryInitialization.java changes) must keep DirectoryInitialization.areDolphinDirectoriesReady(), getDolphinDirectoriesState() and the nested DirectoryInitializationState accessible to this Kotlin class. Upstream keeps them, but check when integrating G180. G027 doesn't depend on G180.
- **Commits:**
  - `79f0c814e4` Android: Convert AfterDirectoryInitializationRunner to Kotlin — **MEDIUM**: An API-compatible conversion of a class called from XR-added (FirstLaunchDialogs.kt) and XR-modified (MainPresenter.kt, EmulationActivity.kt, etc.) Android files. Rule 6.

### G032 — PR #14442 — PerformanceMetrics: Use HookableEvent for state changed callback

- **Subsystem:** VideoCommon/PerformanceMetrics + Core state-change notification (Core.cpp)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-12 (merge `12d790a54a`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Core/Core/Core.cpp`, `Source/Core/VideoCommon/PerformanceMetrics.cpp`, `Source/Core/VideoCommon/PerformanceMetrics.h`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Source/Core/Core/Core.cpp`
- **Upstream intent:** Remove the special-case direct call g_perf_metrics.OnEmulationStateChanged() from Core::NotifyStateChanged. Instead, PerformanceMetrics registers a normal Core::AddOnStateChangedCallback EventHook in a new constructor, and that hook invalidates the FPS/VPS trackers' last time. PerformanceMetrics.h now includes Common/HookableEvent.h instead of Core/Core.h.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** Applies cleanly, and the Core.cpp hunk (NotifyStateChanged) is separate from XR's Core.cpp edits. On its own, though, it creates a static-initialization-order hazard. The global g_perf_metrics, defined in the videocommon lib, now calls Core::AddOnStateChangedCallback() from its constructor during static init. That calls s_state_changed_event.Register(), which locks m_storage->listeners_mutex. m_storage is a dynamically initialized std::make_shared member of a static in Core.cpp, in the core lib. Static-init order across libraries is unspecified on both MSVC and the NDK, so this can crash at startup on a null deref. Upstream fixed exactly this two days later in f9c3f06f0a (G038, 'avoids the static initialization order fiasco between Core and VideoCommon'). It is not a cosmetic change in an XR-modified file, and it must never be auto-picked without G038, so MEDIUM.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** Source/Core/Core/Core.cpp: upstream only edits NotifyStateChanged(), dropping the g_perf_metrics.OnEmulationStateChanged call and adding a const parameter. XR's Core.cpp edits are elsewhere: GraphicsSettings/OpenXRManager includes, RegisterCurrentThreadWithOpenXR, and PinEmulationThreadToPerformanceCore in CpuThread, FifoPlayerThread and the video thread. No XR code calls OnEmulationStateChanged or g_perf_metrics: git grep at 5e9a304a65 finds only upstream call sites. Dropping Core/Core.h from PerformanceMetrics.h is safe because every includer (Core.cpp, CoreTiming.cpp, SystemTimers.cpp, VideoInterface.cpp, OnScreenUI.cpp) includes what it needs directly.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Apply 252ec7452c immediately followed by f9c3f06f0a (G038) in the same integration step; both apply cleanly in greedy order. Optionally also apply 2aba231915 (G039). Never build or ship G032 without G038. Afterwards, `git grep g_perf_metrics` must return nothing. Build Windows (Build-vs2022) and Quest, then check that the FPS/VPS counters reset properly across pause/resume.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Handle together with:** G038 (Move PerformanceMetrics from global variable to System)
- **Notes:** Apart from the init-order hazard, behavior is the same: counter invalidation now runs as one of the state-changed hooks instead of after all of them.
- **Commits:**
  - `252ec7452c` PerformanceMetrics: Use HookableEvent for state changed callback — **MEDIUM**: Clean and its Core.cpp hunk is separate from XR's edits, but it introduces a static-init-order hazard that only f9c3f06f0a (G038) removes, so it must be applied as a pair.

### G038 — PR #14463 — Move PerformanceMetrics from global variable to System

- **Subsystem:** Core/System + VideoCommon/PerformanceMetrics (call sites in Core.cpp, CoreTiming.cpp, VideoInterface.cpp, SystemTimers.cpp, OnScreenUI.cpp)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-14 (merge `210f70a501`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Core/Core/Core.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/CoreTiming.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/HW/SystemTimers.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/HW/VideoInterface.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/System.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/System.h`, `Source/Core/VideoCommon/OnScreenUI.cpp`, `Source/Core/VideoCommon/PerformanceMetrics.cpp`, `Source/Core/VideoCommon/PerformanceMetrics.h`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Source/Core/Core/Core.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/CoreTiming.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/HW/VideoInterface.cpp`, `Source/Core/VideoCommon/OnScreenUI.cpp`
- **Upstream intent:** Replace the global g_perf_metrics with a PerformanceMetrics member of Core::System::Impl, reached through a new accessor System::GetPerfMetrics(). It is constructed lazily with the function-local-static System singleton, which removes the Core/VideoCommon static-init-order fiasco created by G032's constructor hook. All call sites are updated, and System.h includes VideoCommon/PerformanceMetrics.h.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** A mechanical refactor that applies cleanly once G032 is in; its alone-on-HEAD conflict is only the PerformanceMetrics.cpp context added by G032. It touches four XR-modified files (Core.cpp, CoreTiming.cpp, VideoInterface.cpp, OnScreenUI.cpp), all in hunks separate from XR's edits. No XR code, including Source/Android and VideoCommon/VR, references g_perf_metrics, so there is no compile fallout. It is still MEDIUM by the rubric for three reasons: it is an ownership/lifetime refactor rather than a cosmetic cleanup; it edits frame-pacing files (CoreTiming::SleepUntil/Throttle/AdjustEventQueueTimes, VideoInterface::EndField); and it depends on G032 (MEDIUM).
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** Core.cpp: CpuThread Reset() and Callback_FramePresented(). XR's Android OpenXR thread registration and core pinning sit elsewhere in CpuThread, and XR does not touch Callback_FramePresented. CoreTiming.cpp: perf calls in SleepUntil, Throttle and AdjustEventQueueTimes; XR's VR forced-VBI GetEffectiveTotalOverclockFactor() lives in RefreshConfig, so they are separate. VideoInterface.cpp: CountVBlank in EndField; XR's GetEffectiveVBIOverrideFactor() lives in RefreshConfig, so they are separate. OnScreenUI.cpp: DrawImGuiStats in Finalize(); XR's Shader Flags/Hunting OSD is in DrawDebugText, separate. OnScreenUI.cpp already includes Core/System.h. System.h, System.cpp and SystemTimers.cpp are not XR-modified. The speed and VPS values that the VR forced-VBI modes influence are unchanged; only the object's location moves.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick f9c3f06f0a directly after 252ec7452c (G032). Optionally follow with 2aba231915 (G039, forward declarations in System.h); a grep found no XR-only file that relies on System.h transitively including VideoEvents.h or PerformanceMetrics.h. Verify that `git grep -n g_perf_metrics` is empty. Build Build-vs2022 (ENABLE_VR=ON) and the Quest flavor. Smoke-test the FPS/VPS/speed text and graph in VR and flat, including with a forced VBI frequency.
- **Upstream dependencies:** G032
- **Handle together with:** G032 (PerformanceMetrics: Use HookableEvent for state changed call), G039 (System: Use forward declarations in System.h)
- **Notes:** System.h pulls in PerformanceMetrics.h (HookableEvent and PerformanceTracker; no global name clashes found) until G039 replaces that include with forward declarations.

  - `f9c3f06f0a` Move PerformanceMetrics from global variable to System This avoids the static initialization order fiasco between Core and VideoCommon — **MEDIUM**: Mechanical global-to-System refactor across XR-modified frame-pacing/Core/OSD files, in hunks separate from XR's edits. Requires G032's constructor context.

### G040 — PR #14224 — Externals: Fix clashing dependencies

- **Subsystem:** Build system: CMake dependency selection (CMake/DolphinLibraryTools.cmake, CMake/FindLibUSB.cmake, root CMakeLists.txt) and Externals CMake for minizip-ng, cURL, SDL, libusb
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-15 (merge `a28fff3f00`), 5 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `CMake/DolphinLibraryTools.cmake`, `CMakeLists.txt`, `Externals/minizip-ng/CMakeLists.txt`, `Externals/curl/CMakeLists.txt`, `CMake/FindLibUSB.cmake`, `Externals/SDL/CMakeLists.txt`, `Externals/libusb/CMakeLists.txt`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `CMakeLists.txt`, `Externals/minizip-ng/CMakeLists.txt`
- **Upstream intent:** Make bundled-vs-system dependency handling consistent. (a) <lib>_TYPE is now CACHE INTERNAL and keyed by library name; this also fixes the empty-prefix `_TYPE` written when use_system is OFF. It FATAL_ERRORs when a dependency flips System<->Bundled in an existing build dir. (b) Bundled minizip-ng uses Dolphin's bundled Iconv/LibLZMA/ZLIB/ZSTD targets, with no fetching and no pkg-config, and with BZIP2/PKCRYPT/WZAES/OPENSSL/LIBBSD off. This fixes upstream's Windows CMake build, and the Iconv lookup is moved before minizip-ng. (c) cURL really disables ZLIB/ZSTD. (d) LibUSB goes through FindLibUSB.cmake and always yields an imported target. (e) Bundled SDL uses the bundled libusb: the SDL3 lookup moves after LibUSB, and LibUSB::LibUSB becomes an INTERFACE IMPORTED GLOBAL target. As a side effect this enables SDL's libusb HIDAPI backend on Windows.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** This is a dependency/toolchain change that affects both XR build paths: the Windows VS2022 CMake build (Build-vs2022) and the Quest build (Gradle -> root CMakeLists.txt). Rule 5 therefore rules out LOW. 4af06fd487 textually conflicts with XR's own minizip-ng fix. 4af06fd487's `get_target_property(ZLIB_INCLUDE_DIRS ...)` also caused a Windows-host Android Gradle InvalidPathException that upstream only fixed later in e8da93d52f (G175), and XR builds Quest on Windows via build-quest.ps1/gradlew. The group also changes what gets linked: SDL gains static libusb HIDAPI on Windows, minizip-ng's codec set changes, and curl drops zlib/zstd. It is not HIGH: it is about 100 lines of CMake with no VR/renderer/input code, and the only conflict is small and obvious.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** Externals/minizip-ng/CMakeLists.txt: XR (a2dad38be5) added `set(MZ_FETCH_LIBS OFF CACHE BOOL ... FORCE)` and `set(MZ_FORCE_FETCH_LIBS OFF CACHE BOOL ... FORCE)` to avoid the same Windows-CMake fetch/redefine error. Upstream rewrites that exact region (merge-tree shows a single conflict hunk: XR's 2 lines vs upstream's 6 `set(MZ_*)` lines; the rest merges). Per the generated minizip-ng.vcxproj, XR's current Windows build has HAVE_ZLIB, PKCRYPT and WZAES but no LZMA or ZSTD: with fetch off, minizip's find_package for LZMA/ZSTD fails and it silently drops them. After upstream, ZLIB/LZMA/ZSTD come from the bundled targets and PKCRYPT/WZAES are off; no Dolphin/XR code uses zip encryption. Root CMakeLists.txt hunks (Iconv block moved above pugixml, LibUSB via dolphin_find_optional_system_library, SDL3 lookup moved below LibUSB) are disjoint from XR's ENABLE_VR/OpenXR-loader block (L661-689) and Qt6.5.1 prefix block, and they merge cleanly. The SDL3 lookup will now run after XR's add_subdirectory(Externals/OpenXR), but no XR CMake references SDL/LibUSB/Iconv, and XR's InputCommon/CMakeLists.txt only adds OpenXR sources. No XR CMake reads <lib>_TYPE. XR did not modify DolphinLibraryTools.cmake, FindLibUSB.cmake, or Externals/{SDL,libusb,curl}/CMakeLists.txt.
- **Suggested integration approach:** 1. Apply all five commits in order: 11065e9911, 4af06fd487, 184c6ee068, 3cea68c2e5, c6411a2952. Resolve Externals/minizip-ng/CMakeLists.txt by taking upstream's block: MZ_FETCH_LIBS/BZIP2/PKCRYPT/WZAES/OPENSSL/LIBBSD OFF, the bundled Iconv/LibLZMA/ZLIB/ZSTD pre-seeding, and the PkgConfig disable. Keeping XR's `MZ_FORCE_FETCH_LIBS OFF CACHE ... FORCE` line is harmless, and XR's CACHE-FORCE MZ_FETCH_LIBS is superseded by upstream's plain set, which works because minizip-ng requires CMake 3.13 (CMP0077 NEW). 2. In the same step, apply G175 e8da93d52f (replaces get_target_property(ZLIB_INCLUDE_DIRS) with a $<TARGET_PROPERTY:...> genex) so the Quest Gradle build on Windows does not hit the InvalidPathException. Also apply G042 44e3f3708e, which textually needs 184c6ee068. 3. Reconfigure Build-vs2022 from a fresh CMakeCache.txt. <lib>_TYPE is now cached, and any System<->Bundled flip is a FATAL_ERROR. 4. Check the configure log: 'Using static LibUSB/SDL3/minizip-ng/... from Externals', and minizip-ng reporting ZLIB, LZMA and ZSTD. Build Dolphin.exe with ENABLE_VR=ON. 5. Run build-quest.ps1 and confirm 'Using static Iconv from Externals' in the arm64 configure output. 6. Runtime smoke test on Windows: SDL gamepads plus the GC adapter (SDL now statically links libusb HIDAPI), and loading a resource-pack .zip.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Handle together with:** G175 (Fix android gradle sync error on windows), G042 (cURL: Disable Brotli)
- **Notes:** Android Iconv: upstream's `set(USE_SYSTEM_Iconv OFF)` is a no-op, because dolphin_optional_system_library reads the upper-case USE_SYSTEM_ICONV (this is unchanged in upstream's final tree 771fb154059c). Android therefore goes through find_package(Iconv 1.14) with USE_SYSTEM_LIBS=AUTO. It still falls back to bundled libiconv because XR's minSdk is 21 (<28), which hides bionic's iconv_open. That matters: bionic iconv lacks Shift-JIS. Re-check this if the Quest minSdk is ever raised to 28 or higher. SDL/libusb on Windows: Dolphin already sets SDL_HINT_JOYSTICK_HIDAPI_GAMECUBE to 0 when the GC adapter is configured (InputCommon/ControllerInterface/SDL/SDL.cpp:177, not XR-modified). The OpenXR controller backend is unaffected. XR's Build-vs2022 cache already has LIBUSB_INCLUDE_DIR/LIBUSB_LIBRARIES NOTFOUND and PKG_CONFIG_EXECUTABLE NOTFOUND, so LibUSB will resolve to Bundled on this machine. 184c6ee068's ZLIB_FOUND/ZSTD_FOUND FALSE are scoped to Externals/curl and do not leak.
- **Commits:**
  - `11065e9911` CMake: Error out when switching sources for dependencies — **MEDIUM**: Adds dolphin_set_library_type(): <lib>_TYPE becomes CACHE INTERNAL keyed by library name, fixing the empty-prefix `_TYPE` when use_system is OFF, and it FATAL_ERRORs on a System<->Bundled flip in an existing cache. Clean, XR-untouched, and no XR CMake consumes <lib>_TYPE. It is the prerequisite for 4af06fd487 and c6411a2952's *_TYPE checks. Build-dir behavior changes, so reconfigure from a fresh cache.
  - `4af06fd487` minizip-ng: Properly use libraries from Externals — **MEDIUM**: Conflicts with XR's fetch-disable lines in Externals/minizip-ng/CMakeLists.txt. Rewires minizip-ng to the bundled Iconv/LZMA/ZLIB/ZSTD targets (reading Iconv_TYPE/LibLZMA_TYPE/ZLIB_TYPE/ZSTD_TYPE from 11065e9911) and turns BZIP2/PKCRYPT/WZAES/OPENSSL/LIBBSD off. Moves the Iconv lookup before minizip-ng; the Android path now uses find_package(Iconv) with AUTO. Its get_target_property(ZLIB_INCLUDE_DIRS) breaks Android Gradle on Windows hosts until e8da93d52f (G175).
  - `184c6ee068` cURL: Actually disable ZLIB/ZSTD — **MEDIUM**: Bundled cURL: CURL_ZSTD becomes CACHE FORCE OFF, and ZLIB_FOUND/ZSTD_FOUND FALSE are set in curl's scope so curl never links zlib/zstd even after the root find_package. Clean and XR-untouched, but it is a dependency-configuration change for both the Windows and Quest builds, kept with the PR. G042 44e3f3708e (Brotli) textually builds on it.
  - `3cea68c2e5` LibUSB: Improve library detection — **MEDIUM**: LibUSB now uses dolphin_find_optional_system_library and a reworked CMake/FindLibUSB.cmake: early return on Android or when a LibUSB::LibUSB target exists, and it always creates an imported target when found. The root CMakeLists hunk is disjoint from XR and clean. On XR's Windows cache libusb is not found, so it stays Bundled; Android is unchanged (if NOT ANDROID).
  - `c6411a2952` SDL: Use bundled LibUSB when desirable — **MEDIUM**: Moves the SDL3 lookup below LibUSB, now after XR's ENABLE_VR/OpenXR-loader block (no XR ordering dependency found). Exposes bundled libusb as an INTERFACE IMPORTED GLOBAL LibUSB::LibUSB, and presets LibUSB_FOUND plus SDL_HIDAPI_LIBUSB_SHARED OFF when LibUSB_TYPE is Bundled. Bundled SDL (whose find_package(LibUSB) uses Dolphin's FindLibUSB first) then statically links libusb HIDAPI, which is new on Windows and changes SDL device handling at runtime.

### G041 — PR #14453 — Android: Raise minSdk to 24 (Android 7.0)

- **Subsystem:** Android build configuration (minSdk) + Readme
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-15 (merge `66db206e34`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Readme.md`, `Source/Android/app/build.gradle.kts`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Readme.md`, `Source/Android/app/build.gradle.kts`
- **Upstream intent:** Raise defaultConfig.minSdk from 21 to 24 to drop Android 5.x/6.0 and unblock AndroidX updates such as Lifecycle 2.10 in G063. The Readme requirement text changes to match.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** (1) It is an SDK-level build change, which rule 5 excludes from LOW. (2) Readme.md conflicts: XR rewrote the Readme (+171) and its Android line is at L42 with different list formatting. (3) It touches XR-modified app/build.gradle.kts, but the `minSdk` line doesn't overlap XR's flavorDimensions/productFlavors/releaseNoMinify/releaseDebuggable/abiFilters edits and applies cleanly. (4) XR-specific side effect: AGP derives ANDROID_PLATFORM from minSdk, and there is no explicit override. The quest flavor passes -DENABLE_VR=ON, so the root CMakeLists.txt L680-687 does add_subdirectory(Externals/OpenXR). Externals/OpenXR/src/CMakeLists.txt L81 sets VULKAN_INCOMPATIBLE only when ANDROID_PLATFORM_LEVEL < 24. At 24, the loader sub-build will run find_package(Vulkan) and add_definitions(-DXR_USE_GRAPHICS_API_VULKAN), and loader sources will include the NDK's <vulkan/vulkan.h> via xr_dependencies.h/openxr_platform.h. The loader has no Vulkan-specific logic, and XR code defines XR_USE_GRAPHICS_API_VULKAN itself (VulkanOpenXR.cpp/.h, OpenXRUtilitySession.cpp) and uses only public OpenXR headers, so this is expected to be harmless. Upstream never tested it, though. Quest devices are API 29+, so dropping API 21-23 doesn't matter functionally.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** app/build.gradle.kts: no overlap with XR's quest/standard productFlavors (IS_QUEST buildConfigField, per-flavor ENABLE_VR and abiFilters) or the diagnostic release variants. Readme.md: textual conflict at XR L42 ('- Android (5.0 Lollipop or higher).'). Indirect: the XR-only OpenXR SDK CMake Vulkan gate (Externals/OpenXR/src/CMakeLists.txt L81) flips for the Quest native build. Common/CommonFuncs.cpp's `__ANDROID_API__ >= 23` strerror_r switch also flips, but the preprocessor handles both cases.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick 57a8a78d06. Resolve Readme.md by keeping XR's Readme and changing L42 to '- Android (7.0 Nougat or higher).'. Keep all XR gradle edits; only the minSdk line changes. Delete Source/Android/app/.cxx to force a CMake reconfigure, then run build-quest.ps1. Check that the configure log shows the OpenXR sub-build's 'Enabling Vulkan support' (or that Vulkan simply isn't found; both are fine) and that the openxr_loader static lib compiles. Run a Quest device test: VR session starts on both the Vulkan and GLES backends. Also build standardDebug. Apply G043 right after.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Handle together with:** G043 (Android: Remove version guards for API levels below 23 (Mars)
- **Notes:** This is a fork-policy decision for XR's standard (phone) flavor, with negligible impact. Downstream: G043 (fd20322f5f) was authored directly on top of this PR's merge (66db206e34). G063 (e878174235, version catalog with AGP 9.1.0, Kotlin 2.3.20, Lifecycle 2.10.0) needs minSdk >= 23, according to this commit's message.
- **Commits:**
  - `57a8a78d06` Android: Raise minSdk to 24 (Android 7.0) — **MEDIUM**: An SDK-level change in XR-modified build.gradle.kts with a Readme.md conflict. It also flips the OpenXR SDK's ANDROID_PLATFORM_LEVEL<24 Vulkan gate for the Quest native build.

G063 also switches to proguard-android-optimize.txt, which makes keep rules like this one more important.

### G073 — PR #14470 — Triforce: Check only first byte in region flag.

- **Subsystem:** DiscIO (Triforce boot.id region flag -> game ID country / region)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-03-25 (merge `3c8a72eb18`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Core/DiscIO/VolumeDisc.cpp`, `Source/Core/DiscIO/VolumeGC.cpp`
- **XR-modified files touched:** none
- **Upstream intent:** Mask Triforce boot.id region_flags with 0xFF in VolumeDisc::GetCountry() and VolumeGC::GetRegion(). Dumps whose region byte is followed by 0xFF padding (e.g. Mario Kart Arcade GP 2 USA: 0E FF FF FF) then stop falling into the 'default: JAPAN' case, so USA MKAGP2 becomes GNLE82/NTSC-U instead of GNLJ82/NTSC-J.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** The code change is trivially safe: two lines in DiscIO, which XR never modified, clean alone and in greedy, with no prerequisites. But it changes the game ID that DolphinXR keys VR profiles on. XR ships Data/Sys/GameSettingsVR/GNLJ82.ini for MKAGP2 (UnitsPerMeter=50, VirtualScreen=False, $HUD/$Menu ElementsGroupOverride handling=screen). VR profiles are matched by exact ID, 3-char and 1-char filenames via ConfigLoaders::GetGameIniFilenames, which is called from ConfigManager.cpp LoadVRSettingsFromINI, ElementsGroupManager::LoadOverridesFromINI, ShaderHunter, HideObjectEngine and TextureElementManager. Today, any non-JP MKAGP2 dump (USA 0x0E, Export 0x0C or Asia 0x08 with 0xFF padding) resolves to GNLJ82 and gets the shipped VR profile. After this commit it becomes GNLE82/GNLP82/GNLW82 and silently loses that profile. That is a VR-profile behavior change for a VR-profiled game, which needs a fork data decision rather than an auto-pick.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** No XR source overlap (XR did not touch VolumeDisc.cpp or VolumeGC.cpp). The overlap is in data: Data/Sys/GameSettingsVR/GNLJ82.ini (added by XR commit 49ad800936 'Added Default GamesettingsVR') is looked up by the game ID that this commit changes for non-JP MKAGP2 dumps. User-side User/GameSettingsVR/GNLJ82.ini, User/GameSettings, StateSaves and the Triforce tridimm_/backup_/tricard_<gameid>.bin files (AMMediaboard.cpp:459-460, SI_DeviceAMBaseboard.cpp:162) are keyed by the same ID.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick 3eea3a90b5 as-is; it applies cleanly with no prerequisites. Before or alongside it, the fork owner should check which MKAGP2 dump GNLJ82.ini was authored against (boot.id byte 0x38: 0x02=JP, 0x0E=USA). If it was a non-JP dump, ship the same VR profile under the corrected ID (for example GameSettingsVR/GNLE82.ini, or a region-agnostic GNL.ini). Add a release note telling users to rename their User/GameSettingsVR/GNLJ82.ini and Triforce save files. Integrate this together with G150 (f55001b9d9: upstream GNLE82.ini/GNLJ82.ini AR/Gecko codes). Then check that the VR_CONFIG log shows the profile applied when MKAGP2 boots in VR.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Handle together with:** G150 (GameSettings: Add INI files for Mario Kart Arcade GP 2.)
- **Notes:** GNLJ82 is the only Triforce ID in GameSettingsVR. JP dumps are unaffected (0x02 maps to 'J' before and after). For USA MKAGP2 the region changes from NTSC_J to NTSC_U; both are 60 Hz, so there is no VI or frame-pacing impact. Without this commit, G150's GNLE82.ini can never match, and USA dumps would load GNLJ82.ini's JP-address AR/Gecko codes.
- **Commits:**
  - `3eea3a90b5` Triforce: Check only first byte in region flag. — **MEDIUM**: Changes the computed game ID (GNLJ82 -> GNLE82 for USA MKAGP2) that XR's GameSettingsVR/GNLJ82.ini VR profile is keyed on. The code itself is clean and trivial.
