### G213 — PR #14646 — HW/ProcessorInterface: Add support for all known registers

- **Subsystem:** Core HW / ProcessorInterface (GX CPU-FIFO registers, interrupts) + savestate
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-06-28 (merge `6d796f7863`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Core/Core/HW/ProcessorInterface.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/HW/ProcessorInterface.h`, `Source/Core/Core/State.cpp`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Source/Core/Core/State.cpp`
- **Upstream intent:** Model all known PI registers from hardware testing. Adds PI_ERROR_CAUSE (0x1C, write mask 7), PI_ERROR_ADDRESS (0x20, read-only), PI_UNKNOWN (0x28, init 0x1FF, mask 0x3FF) and PI_FLIPPER_BUS_STRENGTH (0x30, renamed from PI_FLIPPER_UNK, init 0x02492492, mask 0x07FFFFFF), plus an ErrorCause enum. PI_FIFO_BASE/END/WPTR writes change from DirectWrite(mask 0xFFFFFFE0) to ComplexWrite masked by CommandProcessor::GetPhysicalAddressMask(IsWii) & ~0x1F. DoState gains 4 u32 fields. STATE_VERSION 191->192.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** The textual conflict is trivial: merge-tree on XR HEAD shows ONLY the STATE_VERSION line conflicts, and ProcessorInterface.cpp/.h merge clean (XR never touched them). It is still MEDIUM for three reasons. (1) It changes emulated semantics of the CPU-side GX FIFO pointers. GPFifo::UpdateGatherPipe writes through m_fifo_cpu_write_pointer. CommandProcessorManager::GatherPipeBursted compares m_fifo_cpu_base/end with CP base/end to decide FlushGpu() in dual-core (the 'multibuffer' path) and to fire the 'FIFOs linked but out of sync' asserts. This is the FIFO/GPU-sync path that feeds XR's video thread and frame pacing. (2) The savestate layout changes beyond the version line (PI DoState +4 fields), which the rubric lists as MEDIUM. (3) Registers games previously could not read now return non-zero values. For retail software writing physical addresses the new mask is a no-op, so the practical risk is low.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** State.cpp: conflict is limited to 'constexpr u32 STATE_VERSION = 181; // Last changed for VR pane-remap GS constant' vs upstream 192. XR's other State.cpp edits (SaveOptions/compression_type, WaitForPendingSaves, <future>) are untouched. No XR edits in ProcessorInterface.*, GPFifo.cpp or CommandProcessor.cpp. XR's Fifo.cpp only adds a thread name. XR's Android jni/Input/HotkeyDispatcher.cpp includes ProcessorInterface.h only for ResetButton_Tap (unchanged). The new unscoped ErrorCause enumerators (NoError, Reserved, …) are namespaced and collide with nothing in XR translation units (no 'using namespace ProcessorInterface').
- **Suggested integration approach:** Cherry-pick 82a317124a. Resolve State.cpp by keeping XR's line with STATE_VERSION = XR current + 1 (note PR 14646 in the comment). Build Build-vs2022 (ENABLE_VR=ON) and Quest. Test a dual-core GC VR title (GM8E01 or GFZE01) and a Wii VR title (RMGE01 or R3ME01) in VR for several minutes. Confirm no 'FIFOs linked but out of sync' asserts or log spam and no FPS or pacing change. Run a savestate save/load round-trip and FifoPlayer playback of a .dff (FifoPlayer::WritePI writes PI_FIFO_BASE/WPTR). No later upstream commit references the new PI symbols, so deferring or skipping this PR blocks nothing except the upstream STATE_VERSION numbering.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **Notes:** CommandProcessor::GetPhysicalAddressMask already exists at XR HEAD (CommandProcessor.h:155), so there is no code prerequisite. On Quest, JitArm64 inlines DirectWrite MMIO handlers (Jit_Util.cpp MMIOWriteCodeGenerator::VisitDirect). The PI_FIFO_* writes now go through a ComplexWrite call, which is negligible because games write these registers only at FIFO setup. The Init() reorder of m_interrupt_cause is value-neutral.
- **Commits:**
  - `82a317124a` HW/ProcessorInterface: Add support for all known registers — **MEDIUM**: Behavioral change to the GX CPU-FIFO pointer registers (physical-address masking) used by GPFifo and CommandProcessor::GatherPipeBursted FlushGpu/link checks, plus a PI savestate layout change. The only XR conflict is the STATE_VERSION line.

### G214 — PR #14278 — PPCAnalyst: Split "in use" analysis into reads and writes

- **Subsystem:** PowerPC JIT register cache (PPCAnalyst, Jit64 RegCache, JitArm64 RegCache)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-06-28 (merge `02bacff51f`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/Jit64/Jit.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/Jit64/Jit_Integer.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/Jit64/RegCache/GPRRegCache.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/Jit64/RegCache/JitRegCache.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/Jit64/RegCache/JitRegCache.h`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/JitArm64/Jit.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/JitArm64/JitArm64_BackPatch.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/JitArm64/JitArm64_Branch.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/JitArm64/JitArm64_LoadStore.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/JitArm64/JitArm64_RegCache.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/JitArm64/JitArm64_RegCache.h`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/JitArm64/JitArm64_SystemRegisters.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/PPCAnalyst.cpp`, `Source/Core/Core/PowerPC/PPCAnalyst.h`
- **XR-modified files touched:** none
- **Upstream intent:** Performance change. CodeOp gpr/fpr/crInUse is replaced by WillBeRead/WillBeWritten liveness, so a guest register can be written back to ppcState right after its last write instead of after its last read. A new FlushMode::Undirty stores the register but keeps it cached and marks it clean, which also removes duplicate stores on both sides of conditional branches. API churn: Arm64 FlushMode::All is renamed Full and gains Undirty; StoreRegisters/StoreCRRegisters are replaced by public FlushRegisters/FlushCRRegisters(regs, mode, tmp); Jit64 gets RegCache::Flush(pregs, mode, ignore) and MaintainState is renamed Undirty. The lmw/stmw/mfcr flush logic is simplified.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** Mechanically easy. There is no XR overlap, and nothing outside Core/PowerPC references the renamed CodeOp fields, FlushMode or StoreRegisters (git grep at XR HEAD is empty). Greedy is clean. The alone conflict in JitArm64/Jit.cpp is only textual: 058c7021b8 (G168) changed MSRUpdated's TBZ line next to the Flush lines this commit renames. With G168 applied first, merge-tree onto XR HEAD is clean, and merge 02bacff51f has no manual resolution. It is rated MEDIUM rather than LOW because it is not an isolated bug fix. It changes the writeback policy for every compiled block in BOTH JITs (374 changed lines, 14 files), including JitArm64, which runs XR's Quest build. A latent bug would show up as random game misbehaviour that is hard to attribute during VR testing. No upstream follow-up fixes or reverts exist in range through 771fb15405; only G227 builds on it.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** none. XR has no edits under Source/Core/Core/PowerPC. XR's CullingCodeFinder uses PPCAnalyst::FindFunctions, PPCSymbolDB and JitInterface::InvalidateICache, and this commit changes none of them (it only changes PPCAnalyzer::Analyze liveness and the regcaches).
- **Suggested integration approach:** Integrate after 058c7021b8 (G168): cherry-pick 0f72b69dc7, then e68a464efd (G227), as one unit. No conflicts are expected, and merge-tree gives the identical tree whether this runs before or after all the LOW commits of this batch. Build Windows/VS2022 (Jit64) and Quest (JitArm64). Then smoke-test a few titles on PC and on Quest, e.g. MKWii and a Metroid Prime title: boot plus some gameplay. If regressions appear, retest with the JIT register cache off or the Interpreter, and back out G214+G227 together.
- **Upstream dependencies:** G168
- **Handle together with:** G227 (Jit64: Early flush in ComputeRC even when `needs_test`)
- **Notes:** Independent of G216 (cmpXX), verified in both orders. The CodeOp struct layout changes in PPCAnalyst.h, but XR has no consumers of the changed fields. MEDIUM is a conservative call (when uncertain, choose the higher risk): under the strict rubric text the commit is not in an XR caution area, but it is a sizeable codegen-policy change that includes ARM64.
- **Commits:**
  - `0f72b69dc7` PPCAnalyst: Split "in use" analysis into reads and writes — **MEDIUM**: Broad JIT register-cache writeback change on Jit64 and JitArm64 (Quest). No XR overlap. Needs G168 for a textual conflict in JitArm64/Jit.cpp MSRUpdated, and is clean after it.

### G220 — PR #14577 — GameSettings: Disable fast depth calc to fix missing text and allow immediate XFB in Def Jam: Fight for NY.

- **Subsystem:** Data/Sys/GameSettings (game INI)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-06-28 (merge `f2dd20ca40`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Data/Sys/GameSettings/GNW.ini`
- **XR-modified files touched:** none
- **Upstream intent:** The missing menu text in Def Jam: Fight for NY (GNWE69/GNWP69) was caused by fast depth calc, not by immediate XFB. The commit sets [Video_Settings] FastDepthCalc=False, stops forcing ImmediateXFBEnable=False, and cleans up the header and empty sections.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** Data only, XR never modified GameSettings, clean alone and greedy. It removes an XFB forcing and adds a depth-precision setting on GNW, which has no VR profile and is not Metroid, so rule 7 gives LOW with a note. No code prerequisite: the forced-off line was a mechanical 2017 conversion (c58010404d), and this is part of Jordan Woyak's independent ImmediateXFB INI sweep.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** No file overlap. Behavioral: XR defaults ImmediateXFBEnable=true on Quest (GraphicsSettings.cpp DEFAULT_IMMEDIATE_XFB under __ANDROID__&&ENABLE_VR, and QuestVrSettings.kt applyRecommendedDefaults() writes it true globally). On Quest, GNW therefore moves from forced deferred XFB to immediate XFB, and VideoConfig::VRLockHeadPosePerFrame() (= !bImmediateXFB) becomes false for it; that is XR's default Quest path. On PC the default is false, so nothing changes unless the user enabled Immediate XFB. FastDepthCalc=False switches to per-pixel depth. XR's GeometryShaderGen VR paths have explicit !host_config.fast_depth_calc branches, and PixelShaderGen already forces per_pixel_depth for Exact Screen Depth, so this path is supported.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Auto cherry-pick cb9f8a2b04. If a GNW regression is reported on Quest, a user can restore the old behavior with ImmediateXFBEnable=False in their user game INI.
- **Upstream dependencies:** none identified
- **INI video-setting change:** yes (EFB/XFB/copy/vertex-rounding settings)
- **Notes:** XFB and depth INI change on a game without a VR profile. Related but independent sweep groups (different files): G119, G128, G138, G154. Those touch VR-profile games (GAU, GNJ, GHQ) and deserve a separate review of the Quest immediate-XFB default.
- **Commits:**
  - `cb9f8a2b04` GameSettings: Disable fast depth calc to fix missing text and allow immediate XFB in Def Jam: Fight for NY. — **MEDIUM**: GNW.ini only (no VR profile): drops the forced ImmediateXFB=False and adds FastDepthCalc=False. Clean, no code dependency. On Quest the game follows XR's Immediate XFB=true default.
- **Adversarial-review downgrades:**
  - skeptic (all) on `cb9f8a2b04`: LOW → MEDIUM: Concrete VR regression. This is the only commit in the whole upstream range that forces FastDepthCalc=False (git log -S on GameSettings), and GNW.ini is the only INI in the target tree with it (XR HEAD has none; only 3 INIs force it True). It therefore turns on a VR path never shipped before, and that path is broken on XR's default Vulkan multiview path. With fast depth off, PixelShaderGen.cpp:205 enables per_pixel_depth for every depth-tested draw. PixelShaderGen.cpp:1100 and UberShaderPixel.cpp:1003 then compute zCoord = farZ + (clipPos.z/clipPos.w)*zRange (zbias[1] = farZ/zRange, PixelShaderManager.cpp:134-135), which expects console NDC n in [-1,0]. Upstream's VS and XR's GS world branch (GeometryShaderGen.cpp:339-340) store clipPos BEFORE the depth remap. XR's multiview VS does it the other way round: GenerateVRMultiviewVSProjection (VertexShaderGen.cpp:1027-1028; uber UberShaderVertex.cpp:427) runs before 'o.clipPos = o.pos' (VertexShaderGen.cpp:1031-1032; UberShaderVertex.cpp:429-432), and it has already applied o.pos.z = o.pos.w*cvr_depth.w - o.pos.z*cvr_depth.z (default (1,0), so z' = -z; VertexShaderGen.cpp:434-436). The result is clipPos.z/w = -n and zCoord = farZ - n*zRange, which lies in [farZ, farZ+zRange]. With the usual full-range viewport (farZ = zRange = 0xFFFFFF), every 3D fragment clamps to 0xFFFFFF and gets the same depth, so occlusion collapses to draw order; otherwise the depth order is inverted. The GS pane else-branch and head-locked HUD branch (GeometryShaderGen.cpp:303-304, 394-395) also store post-remap z. vk_multiview (ShaderGenCommon.cpp:81) is on for Vulkan whenever MSAA=1 (UseVulkanMultiview defaults true), and QuestVrSettings.applyRecommendedDefaults forces Vulkan. So Def Jam: Fight for NY, which renders correctly in VR today with fast depth, would get broken 3D depth on Quest and PC Vulkan VR. It also loses early-Z, which adds Quest GPU cost. The classifier's 'Exact Screen Depth forces per_pixel_depth' argument covers only 2D screen draws. The ImmediateXFB removal half is benign (Quest then uses XR's default immediate path). Fix XR first (capture clipPos before the remap inside GenerateVRMultiviewVSProjection, mirroring the GS) or take this commit knowingly. Builds and tests cannot catch this.

Check that `git grep -nE 'GetVersion\(\)\|SupportsExtension\(' Source/Android` returns nothing. Build assembleQuestDebug and confirm the Stereoscopy entry still appears under Enhancements on Quest.

- **Upstream intent:** Removes SerialInterfaceManager::ChangeDevice and m_desired_device_types. SI::UpdateDevices, which VideoInterface calls at every SI poll, now reads Config::GetInfoForSIDevice(i), so any config change hot-swaps the SI device. On Android this makes GC ports changeable while running (MAIN_SI_DEVICE_0..3 are dropped from NOT_RUNTIME_EDITABLE). NetPlay and Movie overrides of SI devices and Wii Remote sources move into the NetPlay and Movie config layers: NetSettings gains pad_map/gba_config/wiimote_map/local_player_id, and NetPlayClient::UpdateDevices plus the Config::SetCurrent Wii Remote overrides are removed. Movie::ChangeWiiPads loses its parameter, and GamecubeControllersWidget no longer calls ChangeDevice.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** XR's only file overlap is IntSetting.kt, and the hunks are disjoint: XR added GFX_COMMAND_BUFFERS_IN_FLIGHT near line 100, upstream edits NOT_RUNTIME_EDITABLE_ARRAY around line 196. Once 32eaf7961f (G191) is present it applies cleanly; the alone-conflict is only G191's bounds checks in NetPlayClient::OnPadData/OnPadHostData. It is still a behavioural change in input/config plumbing that VR relies on. XR added WiimoteSource::OpenXR (value 3), which HW/Wiimote.cpp OnSourceChanged/UpdateOpenXRInputOverride, the WiimoteEmu IR-raycast checks and MainWindow::ShowTASInput all treat as a first-class source. The new MovieConfigLoader::LoadFromDTM writes WiimoteSource::Emulated for every used Wii Remote into the Movie layer. That layer is active during playback and, through BeginRecordingInput, during recording too, and dcf9ca05db (G324) makes SaveToDTM count OpenXR as used. So during TAS recording/playback in VR the OpenXR Wii Remote is downgraded to plain Emulated and its OpenXR input override is cleared. The old code overrode only when the controller set changed. NetPlay already forced Emulated before (NetPlayClient::StartGame SetCurrent), so it is unchanged for XR. Upstream's version also has a regression, fixed later by dcf9ca05db (G324): starting an input recording resets all controllers to None.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** IntSetting.kt: disjoint hunks, auto-merges. Semantic overlap with XR's WiimoteSource::OpenXR: HW/Wiimote.cpp IsEmulatedSource/OnSourceChanged/UpdateOpenXRInputOverride/GetOpenXRWiiRemoteState, WiimoteEmu `== WiimoteSource::OpenXR` checks, DolphinQt MainWindow::ShowTASInput. The Movie/NetPlay layers now override that source to Emulated. XR's QuestVrSettings.applyDefaultControllerSetup writes MAIN_SI_DEVICE_0=6 and WIIMOTE_1_SOURCE=3 from the main menu, not at runtime, which is compatible with SI devices becoming runtime-editable. Wiimote.cpp:604 calls movie.ChangeWiiPads() with no arguments, so the new signature is compatible. No XR code calls SI ChangeDevice. No GameSettingsVR profile sets PadType or Wiimote sources.
- **Suggested integration approach:** Integrate after G191 (32eaf7961f) and in the same step as G324 (dcf9ca05db, the movie-recording controller reset fix). Both then apply cleanly. Decide whether the Movie and NetPlay layers should keep XR's WiimoteSource::OpenXR, i.e. write OpenXR instead of Emulated when the lower-layer value is OpenXR in MovieConfigLoader::LoadFromDTM and NetPlayConfigLayerLoader. Make that change as an adaptation of the upstream hunks; do not touch XR's Wiimote.cpp. Test on PC VR with the OpenXR Wii Remote: input recording start/stop and playback (IR pointer must keep working), a GC port change while running (Qt), and a NetPlay session. On Quest, change a GC port from in-game settings.
- **Upstream dependencies:** 32eaf7961f (G191)
- **Handle together with:** G324 (Core: Fix controllers resetting when starting input recordin)
- **Notes:** No savestate impact: m_desired_device_types was never serialized in SI::DoState. SI::UpdateDevices now does 4 cached Config::Get calls per SI poll (about 120 Hz) at a VI site that already reads config; the performance cost is negligible. NetPlayConfigLoader now includes InputCommon/GCAdapter.h, which is already a Core dependency.
- **Commits:**
  - `200e26c98a` Core: Remove SerialInterfaceManager::ChangeDevice — **MEDIUM**: Behavioural change to SI/Wii Remote source overrides (config layers) that interacts with XR's WiimoteSource::OpenXR during Movie recording/playback. Needs G191 textually and G324 to fix the regression.

### G233 — PR #13216 — Core: Implement automatic symbol demangling

- **Subsystem:** Common/SymbolDB + DolphinQt debugger (CodeWidget, Symbols menu, Qt Settings)
- **Landed upstream:** 2026-07-05 (merge `59d7547c2c`), 1 commit(s)
- **Files affected:** `Externals/licenses.md`, `Source/Core/Common/CMakeLists.txt`, `Source/Core/Common/CWDemangler.cpp`, `Source/Core/Common/CWDemangler.h`, `Source/Core/Common/StringUtil.cpp`, `Source/Core/Common/StringUtil.h`, `Source/Core/Common/SymbolDB.cpp`, `Source/Core/Common/SymbolDB.h`, `Source/Core/DolphinLib.props`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Debugger/CodeWidget.cpp`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Debugger/CodeWidget.h`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/MenuBar.cpp`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/MenuBar.h`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Settings.cpp`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Settings.h`, `Source/UnitTests/Common/CMakeLists.txt`, `Source/UnitTests/Common/CWDemanglerTest.cpp`, `Source/UnitTests/UnitTests.vcxproj`
- **XR-modified files touched:** `Source/Core/Common/StringUtil.h`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Settings.cpp`, `Source/Core/DolphinQt/Settings.h`
- **Upstream intent:** Adds Common/CWDemangler.cpp/.h, a C++ port of encounter/cwdemangle (CC0), to demangle Metrowerks CodeWarrior C++ symbol names. Symbol::Rename now stores the result in a new `demangled_name` field, and Symbol::GetDisplayName() picks the name to show. Adds a QSettings key `debugger/showdemanglednames` with Settings::SetShowDemangledNames/IsShowDemangledNames and a ShowDemangledNamesChanged signal, plus a checkable 'Show Demangled Names' action in the Symbols menu. CodeWidget's symbol, calls and callers lists use the display name. Also adds StringUtil StripLeadingWhitespace/StripTrailingWhitespace, unit tests, a licenses.md entry and DolphinLib.props/UnitTests.vcxproj entries.
- **Why not integrated automatically:** This is a debugger-only feature outside the XR caution areas, and greedy_xr is clean. Two things stop it from being LOW. (1) Applied alone on XR HEAD it conflicts in DolphinQt/Settings.h; merge-tree confirms this is the only conflict. Upstream's hunk is anchored on `void SetDebugFont(const QFont& font);`, which comes from 95dec13203 (G139, the std::move/const-ref sweep). G139 touches 12 XR-modified files, including CoreTiming.cpp, ShaderCache.cpp, TextureCacheBase.cpp and ControllerInterface.cpp, so it is expected to be MEDIUM. XR HEAD still has `void SetDebugFont(QFont font);`. (2) It adds new API to three XR-modified files (StringUtil.h, Settings.h, Settings.cpp). That is additive, not a pure cleanup, although the hunks are disjoint from XR's edits. Result: a small, obvious conflict, so MEDIUM. It becomes a clean pick (effectively LOW) if G139 is integrated first.
- **Conflicting / overlapping DolphinXR code:** StringUtil.h: upstream adds two declarations after StripWhitespace (~line 51). XR's only edit in this file is the ASCII fast path for ToLower/ToUpper (~line 300). The hunks are disjoint and CWDemangler doesn't use ToLower/ToUpper. Settings.h/.cpp: XR added NotifyShaderOverridesChanged/NotifyTextureElementOverridesChanged and the ShaderOverridesChanged/TextureElementOverridesChanged signals (Settings.h ~104/~202, Settings.cpp ~479). Upstream adds Set/IsShowDemangledNames after SetDebugFont (Settings.h ~178/~225, Settings.cpp ~871). These are disjoint; the only conflict is the G139 SetDebugFont line. Behavior: XR's CullingCodeFinder (VideoCommon/CullingCodeFinder.cpp, BuildCandidatesFromMap ~line 844) iterates PPCSymbolDB and reads `symbol.name`. Upstream leaves `name` mangled and stores the demangled form in the new `demangled_name` field, so XR's symbol_filter matching and SaveSymbolMap output are unchanged. LoadMap now runs the demangler once per symbol, a minor cost. MenuBar, CodeWidget, SymbolDB, StringUtil.cpp and Common/CMakeLists.txt are not XR-modified.
- **Suggested integration approach:** 1. Integrate after 95dec13203 (G139). Then cherry-pick 3f5460a5ff; the greedy simulation shows it applies cleanly. 2. If G139 is deferred, cherry-pick anyway and resolve Settings.h by keeping XR's `void SetDebugFont(QFont font);` and appending `void SetShowDemangledNames(bool enabled);` and `bool IsShowDemangledNames() const;`. The signal hunk merges cleanly. 3. Follow with e407f178ba (G312), which caches m_show_demangled_names in CodeWidget and fixes the slow per-symbol QSettings lookup this PR introduced. 4. Also take f1a639b12b (from G274), which makes the CWDemangler helpers static for -Wmissing-declarations. 5. The DolphinLib.props and UnitTests.vcxproj hunks are irrelevant to XR's CMake build but apply cleanly. 6. Build Windows (Build-vs2022, ENABLE_VR=ON) and Quest. Common/CMakeLists.txt adds CWDemangler.cpp to the NDK build. It only uses C++20 features (std::ranges::none_of, map::contains, string_view iterator-pair constructor), which VS2022 and NDK r29 support. 7. Smoke test: toggle Symbols > Show Demangled Names, then run one CullingCodeFinder map scan.
- **Upstream dependencies:** 95dec13203 (G139)
- **Handle together with:** G312 (Debugger: Fix symbol search speed), G274 (Resolve various warnings)
- **Notes:** From G274 ('Resolve various warnings', 6 commits), only f1a639b12b concerns CWDemangler. G312 (e407f178ba) explicitly fixes a regression from #13216. The new setting lives in QSettings, not the Dolphin INI.
- **Commits:**
  - `3f5460a5ff` Core: Implement automatic symbol demangling — **MEDIUM**: Debugger-only demangler feature. Without G139 there is a one-line context conflict in Settings.h (SetDebugFont signature). It also adds API in hunks of XR-modified StringUtil.h and Settings.h/.cpp that are disjoint from XR's edits.
